========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I mean, I guess I'm here because things have just been really, really intense lately, especially at work. I buy for a retail chain, you know, wholesale stuff, mostly electronics and accessories right now, and it’s a lot of pressure, which I can usually handle, but... lately, I just can't shake this feeling, this really strong, overwhelming feeling that people are messing with me. Specifically, I think people at work, and honestly, even some of the external vendors we deal with, are secretly tracking my buys. Like, they're watching every single purchase order I put through, every negotiation, and then they're intentionally messing with the product launch numbers. They're manipulating the data to make it look like I'm making terrible decisions, just to make me look stupid in front of corporate. It's so frustrating because I know what I bought and I know the market trends, but the numbers they present just don't add up to reality. I tried to talk to my supervisor about it, I really did, I sat down and tried to explain the whole thing, how the vendors must be colluding with our internal analysts, but he just pulled up these spreadsheets and reports and kept saying they don't prove anything, that the numbers are accurate. But I just know they're not. I know they're doing this to me, and it's driving me crazy trying to figure out how they're pulling it off without leaving a paper trail that he can see. It's just... it's a lot to deal with on top of everything else, you know?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, like I was trying to explain before, it absolutely has to be an organized plan. It is not just a random coincidence or one guy having a grudge. They are specifically targeting my accounts to make me look completely incompetent. I mean, I will spend weeks negotiating a massive shipment of, say, Bluetooth speakers or charging banks, and I know exactly what the margins are supposed to be because I know this industry. But then, somehow, the vendors and the internal guys coordinate to just completely tank the launch numbers. It is a totally orchestrated effort to make me look stupid in front of the corporate executives. 

And the most maddening part, the thing that really drove me to finally come in and talk to someone today, is that I just can't shake this feeling no matter what my supervisor says. I sit in his office, and I lay it all out for him, step by step, how they are tracking my buys and altering the data before it hits the main server. And he just sighs and points at his computer screen and pulls up these supposedly official spreadsheets. He tells me the reports do not prove anything I am saying, that the numbers are completely accurate and I just need to accept the data. 

But of course the reports do not show it! That is the whole point of them working together, they scrub the data so my boss thinks I am just making excuses for bad buys. But I am a good buyer, I barely even finished high school but I worked my way up because I know my job, and I know they are doing this to me. It is just a constant, everyday thing where I am looking over my shoulder, analyzing every email, wondering how they are going to mess with my next purchase order and how deep this whole network really goes.

Well, I mean, as far as feeling down or hopeless... I don't know, maybe sometimes? But honestly, the energetic part, that's definitely something I've dealt with. It's actually been happening a lot lately, especially with all this stuff going on at work. There have been plenty of weeks, I mean, entire weeks, where I sleep maybe three or four hours a night. And it's not like I'm tossing and turning or feeling exhausted the next day. I wake up, and I am just completely wired. I feel this intense surge of energy, like my brain is already running a million miles an hour before I even get out of bed. 

I'll just be lying there, and I start thinking about all the purchase orders I need to review, the vendor contracts, the emails I need to send to try and catch them slipping up. And suddenly, lying in bed just feels like this massive waste of time. I start thinking, "Why am I just lying here when I could be making moves?" I could be analyzing the market trends, I could be cross-referencing the shipping manifests with the internal reports to find the discrepancies they're trying to hide. So I just get up. I'll be up at 3:00 AM, pacing around my apartment, writing down ideas, drafting emails that I wait to send until 8:00 AM so I don't look completely insane. 

But my thoughts are just racing, you know? It's like one idea connects to another, and then another, and I'm putting all these pieces together about how the vendors are manipulating the data. And the energy is just so intense, I don't even feel tired. I feel like I could go for days on just a few hours of sleep because there's so much I need to figure out and so much I need to do to protect my job. It's like I have to be on high alert constantly, so my body just adapts and stops needing sleep. It's crazy, but it's the only way I can keep up with everything they're throwing at me.

Well, yeah, actually. I mean, it's not just the energy and the racing thoughts about work, though that's a huge part of it, obviously. But there are these stretches... these periods of time where I just feel incredible. Like, absolutely on top of the world. It's hard to explain, but it feels like the whole city is just opening up for me, you know? Like I'm completely dialed into the rhythm of everything around me, and I can see all the connections and possibilities that other people are completely blind to. I'll walk down the street, and I just feel this immense power and clarity, like I'm exactly where I'm supposed to be, doing exactly what I'm supposed to be doing, and nobody can touch me. 

But then, the flip side of that is... I can get so irritable. Like, incredibly short-tempered. Because when I'm feeling that good, when my brain is firing on all cylinders and I'm seeing the big picture so clearly, it's agonizing dealing with people who just aren't on my level. If someone at work questions a purchase order I've put together, or if my supervisor tells me I need to slow down or double-check my numbers... I just snap. And I snap way harder than I mean to. I'll just go off on them, because how can they not see what I'm seeing? How can they be so slow and bureaucratic when I've already figured out the whole market trajectory? 

I remember this one time, just a few weeks ago, I was feeling so incredibly sharp, just untouchable, and one of the logistics guys sent me an email asking for clarification on a shipping route I'd selected for a huge batch of portable chargers. And I just lost it. I marched right over to his desk and just laid into him in front of half the office, telling him he was slowing down the entire operation and if he couldn't keep up, he needed to find another job. I felt completely justified in the moment, because he was just being an obstacle, but looking back... I guess I was pretty aggressive. But it's just so frustrating when I'm feeling that elevated, that expansive, and people keep trying to drag me down into their petty little details and doubts. It's like trying to run a marathon while people are throwing sandbags at your feet. So yeah, I get irritable. Very irritable.

It is not just a random coincidence or one guy having a grudge. They are specifically targeting my accounts to make me look completely incompetent. I mean, I will spend weeks negotiating a massive shipment of, say, Bluetooth speakers or charging banks, and I know exactly what the margins are supposed to be because I know this industry. But then, somehow, the vendors and the internal guys coordinate to just completely tank the launch numbers. It is a totally orchestrated effort to make me look stupid in front of the corporate executives. \n\nAnd the most maddening part, the thing that really drove me to finally come in and talk to someone today, is that I just can't shake this feeling no matter what my supervisor says. I sit in his office, and I lay it all out for him, step by step, how they are tracking my buys and altering the data before it hits the main server. And he just sighs and points at his computer screen and pulls up these supposedly official spreadsheets. He tells me the reports do not prove anything I am saying, that the numbers are completely accurate and I just need to accept the data. \n\nBut of course the reports do not show it! That is the whole point of them working together, they scrub the data so my boss thinks I am just making excuses for bad buys. It is just a constant, everyday thing where I am looking over my shoulder, analyzing every email, wondering how they are going to mess with my next purchase order and how deep this whole network really goes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced periods where you felt extremely down, hopeless, or lost interest in things for several days or more? Or times when you felt unusually energetic, needed less sleep, or had racing thoughts?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, as far as feeling down or hopeless... I don't know, maybe sometimes? But honestly, the energetic part, that's definitely something I've dealt with. It's actually been happening a lot lately, especially with all this stuff going on at work. There have been plenty of weeks, I mean, entire weeks, where I sleep maybe three or four hours a night. And it's not like I'm tossing and turning or feeling exhausted the next day. I wake up, and I am just completely wired. I feel this intense surge of energy, like my brain is already running a million miles an hour before I even get out of bed. \n\nI'll just be lying there, and I start thinking about all the purchase orders I need to review, the vendor contracts, the emails I need to send to try and catch them slipping up. And suddenly, lying in bed just feels like this massive waste of time. I start thinking, \"Why am I just lying here when I could be making moves?\" I could be analyzing the market trends, I could be cross-referencing the shipping manifests with the internal reports to find the discrepancies they're trying to hide. So I just get up. I'll be up at 3:00 AM, pacing around my apartment, writing down ideas, drafting emails that I wait to send until 8:00 AM so I don't look completely insane. \n\nBut my thoughts are just racing, you know? It's like one idea connects to another, and then another, and I'm putting all these pieces together about how the vendors are manipulating the data. It's like I have to be on high alert constantly, so my body just adapts and stops needing sleep. I mean, it's not just the energy and the racing thoughts about work, though that's a huge part of it, obviously. But there are these stretches... these periods of time where I just feel incredible. Like, absolutely on top of the world. It's hard to explain, but it feels like the whole city is just opening up for me, you know? Like I'm completely dialed into the rhythm of everything around me, and I can see all the connections and possibilities that other people are completely blind to. I'll walk down the street, and I just feel this immense power and clarity, like I'm exactly where I'm supposed to be, doing exactly what I'm supposed to be doing, and nobody can touch me. \n\nBut then, the flip side of that is... I can get so irritable. Like, incredibly short-tempered. Because when I'm feeling that good, when my brain is firing on all cylinders and I'm seeing the big picture so clearly, it's agonizing dealing with people who just aren't on my level. If someone at work questions a purchase order I've put together, or if my supervisor tells me I need to slow down or double-check my numbers... I just snap. And I snap way harder than I mean to. I'll just go off on them, because how can they not see what I'm seeing? How can they be so slow and bureaucratic when I've already figured out the whole market trajectory? \n\nI remember this one time, just a few weeks ago, I was feeling so incredibly sharp, just untouchable, and one of the logistics guys sent me an email asking for clarification on a shipping route I'd selected for a huge batch of portable chargers. And I just lost it. I marched right over to his desk and just laid into him in front of half the office, telling him he was slowing down the entire operation and if he couldn't keep up, he needed to find another job. I felt completely justified in the moment, because he was just being an obstacle, but looking back... I guess I was pretty aggressive. But it's just so frustrating when I'm feeling that elevated, that expansive, and people keep trying to drag me down into their petty little details and doubts. It's like trying to run a marathon while people are throwing sandbags at your feet. So yeah, I get irritable. A week? Oh, man, a week is... I mean, that's nothing. That is just a drop in the bucket compared to what I've actually been dealing with. It's funny you ask if it's just a week, because when I really sit down and look at the timeline—and believe me, I have mapped this out in my head a hundred times while I'm pacing around my living room at 4:00 AM—it goes back way, way further than that. 

Honestly, these... these elevated, irritable, high-energy stretches have been part of my life for about eleven months. Eleven months! It's not just some quick little phase where I have a good week and then it's over, or a couple days of being annoyed. It's been nearly a whole year of this. I can actually trace it back to last spring, right around the time I was putting together this incredibly complex bulk order for smart home hubs, and I just felt this... this massive switch flip inside me. 

And ever since then, for those eleven months, it's just been these long, drawn-out periods where I am completely vibrating with all this power and clarity, and then getting so incredibly frustrated because I have to deal with everyone else dragging their feet and trying to sabotage my numbers. I'll go weeks at a time feeling exactly like this. So, yeah, a week doesn't even begin to cover it. It's been almost a year of living exactly like this, just constantly dialed in, seeing all the angles, and being ready to explode if someone gets in my way or tries to slow down my process.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, exactly, like I was saying earlier... it's exactly like that. I know I touched on it a minute ago, but there have been plenty of weeks—entire stretches of time—where I sleep maybe three or four hours, max. And when I wake up, I am not groggy at all. I don't need to hit snooze, I don't need to drag myself to the coffee maker. I wake up feeling completely wired and ready to go. 

My eyes just snap open, usually around 2:30 or 3:00 in the morning, and instantly, I'm thinking that lying in bed is just wasting time. I mean, I could use that time to make moves, right? Why would I just lay there staring at the ceiling in the dark when I have so much I could be doing? The wholesale market doesn't just stop because it's nighttime here. There are overseas suppliers I could be dealing with, price fluctuations I need to monitor, inventory logs I have to audit before my supervisor even gets to the office. 

Every single minute I spend horizontal just feels like a missed opportunity, or worse, a chance for someone to slip something past me. So the second my eyes open and I feel that buzz—that intense, vibrating energy in my chest—I just know sleep is off the table. I'm wide awake, my brain is already ten steps ahead, and I just have to get up and get to my laptop. It's like my body has just decided that three hours is a full night's rest, and honestly, with how much I get done before the sun even comes up, I really don't see the point of trying to force myself to sleep more anyway.

I mean, it's not just the energy and the racing thoughts about work, though that's a huge part of it, obviously. But there are these stretches... these periods of time where I just feel incredible. Well, I mean, you're asking if people noticed, and I guess... I don't know, it's hard to say what other people really notice, right? Because they're all so wrapped up in their own little worlds, just doing their basic nine-to-five routines, checking boxes, clocking out. They don't have the same level of awareness that I do when I'm in that zone. But, I mean, did they see a change? I guess I haven't really paid that much attention to whether they were looking at me differently for a whole week straight. 

I know I told you about snapping at that logistics guy, and obviously, half the office saw that, so they noticed *that* moment, for sure. But as for them noticing a sustained change in my mood or my intensity for a full seven days... I don't know. People at work just seem to think I'm being demanding or difficult, or maybe they just think I'm really stressed about hitting my quotas for the quarter. They don't really come up to me and say, "Hey, you've been acting incredibly elevated for the past week." They just kind of back off and give me a wide berth when I'm pacing around the breakroom or firing off emails at lightning speed. 

My supervisor did tell me to take a breath once, but that was just a passing comment, not like a formal sit-down where he was tracking my mood for a week. And my family... well, I don't see them as much when I'm this dialed in because I'm just too busy. I have too much going on with the purchasing logs and the overseas vendors to just sit around making small talk with my mom or whatever. So, I don't really know if anyone has specifically clocked it as this week-long change in my whole personality. I'm just operating at a higher level, and if they notice anything, they probably just think I'm finally taking the job as seriously as they all *should* be taking it. So, no, I can't really say for sure if anyone has pointed it out like that.

Oh, man, a week is... I mean, that's nothing. That is just a drop in the bucket compared to what I've actually been dealing with. It's funny you ask if it's just a week, because when I really sit down and look at the timeline—and believe me, I have mapped this out in my head a hundred times while I'm pacing around my living room at 4:00 AM—it goes back way, way further than that. \n\nHonestly, these... these elevated, irritable, high-energy stretches have been part of my life for about eleven months. Eleven months! It's not just some quick little phase where I have a good week and then it's over, or a couple days of being annoyed. It's been nearly a whole year of this. I can actually trace it back to last spring, right around the time I was putting together this incredibly complex bulk order for smart home hubs, and I just felt this... this massive switch flip inside me. \n\nAnd ever since then, for those eleven months, it's just been these long, drawn-out periods where I am completely vibrating with all this power and clarity, and then getting so incredibly frustrated because I have to deal with everyone else dragging their feet and trying to sabotage my numbers. I'll go weeks at a time feeling exactly like this. So, yeah, a week doesn't even begin to cover it. It's been almost a year of living exactly like this, just constantly dialed in, seeing all the angles, and being ready to explode if someone gets in my way or tries to slow down my process.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods where you needed very little sleep—like just a few hours a night—but still felt completely wide awake and full of energy the next day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly, like I was saying earlier... it's exactly like that. I know I touched on it a minute ago, but there have been plenty of weeks—entire stretches of time—where I sleep maybe three or four hours, max. And when I wake up, I am not groggy at all. I don't need to hit snooze, I don't need to drag myself to the coffee maker. I wake up feeling completely wired and ready to go. \n\nMy eyes just snap open, usually around 2:30 or 3:00 in the morning, and instantly, I'm thinking that lying in bed is just wasting time. I mean, I could use that time to make moves, right? Why would I just lay there staring at the ceiling in the dark when I have so much I could be doing? The wholesale market doesn't just stop because it's nighttime here. There are overseas suppliers I could be dealing with, price fluctuations I need to monitor, inventory logs I have to audit before my supervisor even gets to the office. \n\nEvery single minute I spend horizontal just feels like a missed opportunity, or worse, a chance for someone to slip something past me. So the second my eyes open and I feel that buzz—that intense, vibrating energy in my chest—I just know sleep is off the table. I'm wide awake, my brain is already ten steps ahead, and I just have to get up and get to my laptop. It's like my body has just decided that three hours is a full night's rest, and honestly, with how much I get done before the sun even comes up, I really don't see the point of trying to force myself to sleep more anyway.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when your mood was so high, so irritable, or so intense that other people noticed a real change in you—one that lasted for at least a full week?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, you're asking if people noticed, and I guess... I don't know, it's hard to say what other people really notice, right? Because they're all so wrapped up in their own little worlds, just doing their basic nine-to-five routines, checking boxes, clocking out. They don't have the same level of awareness that I do when I'm in that zone. But, I mean, did they see a change? I guess I haven't really paid that much attention to whether they were looking at me differently for a whole week straight. \n\nI know I told you about snapping at that logistics guy, and obviously, half the office saw that, so they noticed *that* moment, for sure. But as for them noticing a sustained change in my mood or my intensity for a full seven days... I don't know. People at work just seem to think I'm being demanding or difficult, or maybe they just think I'm really stressed about hitting my quotas for the quarter. They don't really come up to me and say, \"Hey, you've been acting incredibly elevated for the past week.\" They just kind of back off and give me a wide berth when I'm pacing around the breakroom or firing off emails at lightning speed. \n\nMy supervisor did tell me to take a breath once, but that was just a passing comment, not like a formal sit-down where he was tracking my mood for a week. And my family... well, I don't see them as much when I'm this dialed in because I'm just too busy. I have too much going on with the purchasing logs and the overseas vendors to just sit around making small talk with my mom or whatever. I'm just operating at a higher level, and if they notice anything, they probably just think I'm finally taking the job as seriously as they all *should* be taking it. So, no, I can't really say for sure if anyone has pointed it out like that.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever felt that people were out to get you, or that you were being watched or followed for reasons that others might not understand?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 22 / wholesale_or_retail_buyer / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"What really made me come in is that I can't shake the feeling that people at work and even some vendors are tracking my buys and messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid, even when my supervisor shows me the reports don't prove that.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, yeah, actually... that's exactly why I ended up coming in here in the first place. I mean, it's not just a feeling, it's this constant, heavy realization that I can't shake. It's like I'm walking around with a target on my back, and I know—I just know—that people at work, and even some of the overseas vendors I've been dealing with, are actively tracking my buys. 

And they're not just tracking them to be helpful or to monitor inventory. They are deliberately messing with the launch numbers. It's so obvious to me. They're altering the data, shifting the margins, just trying to make me look stupid in front of the whole department. I've spent hours going over the spreadsheets, looking at the timestamps, trying to catch the exact moment they go in and sabotage my work. It's like this massive, coordinated effort to undermine my authority and my process, because they know I'm onto them. They know I see the angles they're playing.

I even took it to my supervisor. I printed out all these logs, I highlighted the discrepancies, I laid it all out on his desk. I was like, "Look at this, look at what they're doing to my numbers!" And he just... he looked at me like I was crazy. He pulled up the official reports on his screen and tried to tell me that the data didn't prove anything, that I was just misinterpreting the software updates or whatever excuse he came up with. He showed me these reports that supposedly prove no one is tampering with anything, but I know what I see. I know they're manipulating the system behind my back. It's just so frustrating because I can feel them watching my every move, waiting for me to slip up, and no matter how much evidence I gather, no one else seems to grasp the magnitude of what's actually happening.

It's crazy, but it's the only way I can keep up with everything they're throwing at me.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your mood was extremely elevated, overly irritable, or you felt like you were on top of the world, not just energized or focused on work problems?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, actually. I mean, it's not just the energy and the racing thoughts about work, though that's a huge part of it, obviously. But there are these stretches... these periods of time where I just feel incredible. Like, absolutely on top of the world. It's hard to explain, but it feels like the whole city is just opening up for me, you know? Like I'm completely dialed into the rhythm of everything around me, and I can see all the connections and possibilities that other people are completely blind to. I'll walk down the street, and I just feel this immense power and clarity, like I'm exactly where I'm supposed to be, doing exactly what I'm supposed to be doing, and nobody can touch me. \n\nBut then, the flip side of that is... I can get so irritable. Like, incredibly short-tempered. Because when I'm feeling that good, when my brain is firing on all cylinders and I'm seeing the big picture so clearly, it's agonizing dealing with people who just aren't on my level. If someone at work questions a purchase order I've put together, or if my supervisor tells me I need to slow down or double-check my numbers... I just snap. And I snap way harder than I mean to. I'll just go off on them, because how can they not see what I'm seeing? How can they be so slow and bureaucratic when I've already figured out the whole market trajectory? \n\nI remember this one time, just a few weeks ago, I was feeling so incredibly sharp, just untouchable, and one of the logistics guys sent me an email asking for clarification on a shipping route I'd selected for a huge batch of portable chargers. And I just lost it. I marched right over to his desk and just laid into him in front of half the office, telling him he was slowing down the entire operation and if he couldn't keep up, he needed to find another job. I felt completely justified in the moment, because he was just being an obstacle, but looking back... I guess I was pretty aggressive. But it's just so frustrating when I'm feeling that elevated, that expansive, and people keep trying to drag me down into their petty little details and doubts. It's like trying to run a marathon while people are throwing sandbags at your feet. So yeah, I get irritable. Very irritable.\n\nDoctor: Have these periods of feeling extremely elevated, energetic, or irritable lasted for at least a week or more at a time?\n\nPatient: A week? Oh, man, a week is... I mean, that's nothing. That is just a drop in the bucket compared to what I've actually been dealing with. It's funny you ask if it's just a week, because when I really sit down and look at the timeline—and believe me, I have mapped this out in my head a hundred times while I'm pacing around my living room at 4:00 AM—it goes back way, way further than that. \n\nHonestly, these... these elevated, irritable, high-energy stretches have been part of my life for about eleven months. Eleven months! It's not just some quick little phase where I have a good week and then it's over, or a couple days of being annoyed. It's been nearly a whole year of this. I can actually trace it back to last spring, right around the time I was putting together this incredibly complex bulk order for smart home hubs, and I just felt this... this massive switch flip inside me. \n\nAnd ever since then, for those eleven months, it's just been these long, drawn-out periods where I am completely vibrating with all this power and clarity, and then getting so incredibly frustrated because I have to deal with everyone else dragging their feet and trying to sabotage my numbers. I'll go weeks at a time feeling exactly like this. So, yeah, a week doesn't even begin to cover it. It's been almost a year of living exactly like this, just constantly dialed in, seeing all the angles, and being ready to explode if someone gets in my way or tries to slow down my process.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods where you needed very little sleep—like just a few hours a night—but still felt completely wide awake and full of energy the next day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly, like I was saying earlier... it's exactly like that. I know I touched on it a minute ago, but there have been plenty of weeks—entire stretches of time—where I sleep maybe three or four hours, max. And when I wake up, I am not groggy at all. I don't need to hit snooze, I don't need to drag myself to the coffee maker. I wake up feeling completely wired and ready to go. \n\nMy eyes just snap open, usually around 2:30 or 3:00 in the morning, and instantly, I'm thinking that lying in bed is just wasting time. I mean, I could use that time to make moves, right? Why would I just lay there staring at the ceiling in the dark when I have so much I could be doing? The wholesale market doesn't just stop because it's nighttime here. There are overseas suppliers I could be dealing with, price fluctuations I need to monitor, inventory logs I have to audit before my supervisor even gets to the office. \n\nEvery single minute I spend horizontal just feels like a missed opportunity, or worse, a chance for someone to slip something past me. So the second my eyes open and I feel that buzz—that intense, vibrating energy in my chest—I just know sleep is off the table. I'm wide awake, my brain is already ten steps ahead, and I just have to get up and get to my laptop. It's like my body has just decided that three hours is a full night's rest, and honestly, with how much I get done before the sun even comes up, I really don't see the point of trying to force myself to sleep more anyway.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when your mood was so high, so irritable, or so intense that other people noticed a real change in you—one that lasted for at least a full week?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, you're asking if people noticed, and I guess... I don't know, it's hard to say what other people really notice, right? Because they're all so wrapped up in their own little worlds, just doing their basic nine-to-five routines, checking boxes, clocking out. They don't have the same level of awareness that I do when I'm in that zone. But, I mean, did they see a change? I guess I haven't really paid that much attention to whether they were looking at me differently for a whole week straight. \n\nI know I told you about snapping at that logistics guy, and obviously, half the office saw that, so they noticed *that* moment, for sure. But as for them noticing a sustained change in my mood or my intensity for a full seven days... I don't know. People at work just seem to think I'm being demanding or difficult, or maybe they just think I'm really stressed about hitting my quotas for the quarter. They don't really come up to me and say, \"Hey, you've been acting incredibly elevated for the past week.\" They just kind of back off and give me a wide berth when I'm pacing around the breakroom or firing off emails at lightning speed. \n\nMy supervisor did tell me to take a breath once, but that was just a passing comment, not like a formal sit-down where he was tracking my mood for a week. And my family... well, I don't see them as much when I'm this dialed in because I'm just too busy. I have too much going on with the purchasing logs and the overseas vendors to just sit around making small talk with my mom or whatever. So, I don't really know if anyone has specifically clocked it as this week-long change in my whole personality. I'm just operating at a higher level, and if they notice anything, they probably just think I'm finally taking the job as seriously as they all *should* be taking it. So, no, I can't really say for sure if anyone has pointed it out like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that people were out to get you, or that you were being watched or followed for reasons that others might not understand?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, actually... that's exactly why I ended up coming in here in the first place. I mean, it's not just a feeling, it's this constant, heavy realization that I can't shake. It's like I'm walking around with a target on my back, and I know—I just know—that people at work, and even some of the overseas vendors I've been dealing with, are actively tracking my buys. \n\nAnd they're not just tracking them to be helpful or to monitor inventory. They are deliberately messing with the launch numbers. It's so obvious to me. They're altering the data, shifting the margins, just trying to make me look stupid in front of the whole department. I've spent hours going over the spreadsheets, looking at the timestamps, trying to catch the exact moment they go in and sabotage my work. It's like this massive, coordinated effort to undermine my authority and my process, because they know I'm onto them. They know I see the angles they're playing.\n\nI even took it to my supervisor. I printed out all these logs, I highlighted the discrepancies, I laid it all out on his desk. I was like, \"Look at this, look at what they're doing to my numbers!\" And he just... he looked at me like I was crazy. He pulled up the official reports on his screen and tried to tell me that the data didn't prove anything, that I was just misinterpreting the software updates or whatever excuse he came up with. He showed me these reports that supposedly prove no one is tampering with anything, but I know what I see. I know they're manipulating the system behind my back. It's just so frustrating because I can feel them watching my every move, waiting for me to slip up, and no matter how much evidence I gather, no one else seems to grasp the magnitude of what's actually happening.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No... no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I don't sit around thinking the news anchor is talking to me or that a billboard has some secret code on it. That's... that's not what's happening here. I'm talking about real things, you know? The data, the spreadsheets, the actual vendor communications. That's where the sabotage is. 

I don't need to look for hidden messages on TV because the messages I'm dealing with are right there in my inbox, clear as day, if you just know how to read the supply chain metrics. I mean, I watch the news sometimes, sure, like when I'm checking the overseas markets to see if there's a tariff change or a shipping delay that might affect my buys, but I'm looking at it for facts. I'm looking at the raw data of the global economy. I don't think the ticker at the bottom of the screen is spelling out my name or giving me instructions on how to reorganize the warehouse. 

It's all very practical, very grounded in the reality of the wholesale market. The problem isn't that I'm seeing things that aren't there—it's that I'm the only one seeing the things that *are* there. The discrepancies in the launch numbers, the shifted margins, the timestamps on the log-ins... that's all real, tangible evidence of them messing with my work. So, no, I don't get distracted by random objects or TV shows having special meanings. I don't have time for that kind of nonsense when I have actual, documented proof of people actively trying to ruin my career right in front of me, even if my supervisor is too blind to see it.

It's like I have to be on high alert constantly, so my body just adapts and stops needing sleep. It's crazy, but it's the only way I can keep up with everything they're throwing at me.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your mood was extremely elevated, overly irritable, or you felt like you were on top of the world, not just energized or focused on work problems?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, actually. I mean, it's not just the energy and the racing thoughts about work, though that's a huge part of it, obviously. But there are these stretches... these periods of time where I just feel incredible. Like, absolutely on top of the world. It's hard to explain, but it feels like the whole city is just opening up for me, you know? Like I'm completely dialed into the rhythm of everything around me, and I can see all the connections and possibilities that other people are completely blind to. I'll walk down the street, and I just feel this immense power and clarity, like I'm exactly where I'm supposed to be, doing exactly what I'm supposed to be doing, and nobody can touch me. \n\nBut then, the flip side of that is... I can get so irritable. Like, incredibly short-tempered. Because when I'm feeling that good, when my brain is firing on all cylinders and I'm seeing the big picture so clearly, it's agonizing dealing with people who just aren't on my level. If someone at work questions a purchase order I've put together, or if my supervisor tells me I need to slow down or double-check my numbers... I just snap. And I snap way harder than I mean to. I'll just go off on them, because how can they not see what I'm seeing? How can they be so slow and bureaucratic when I've already figured out the whole market trajectory? \n\nI remember this one time, just a few weeks ago, I was feeling so incredibly sharp, just untouchable, and one of the logistics guys sent me an email asking for clarification on a shipping route I'd selected for a huge batch of portable chargers. Eleven months! It's not just some quick little phase where I have a good week and then it's over, or a couple days of being annoyed. It's been nearly a whole year of this. I can actually trace it back to last spring, right around the time I was putting together this incredibly complex bulk order for smart home hubs, and I just felt this... this massive switch flip inside me. \n\nAnd ever since then, for those eleven months, it's just been these long, drawn-out periods where I am completely vibrating with all this power and clarity, and then getting so incredibly frustrated because I have to deal with everyone else dragging their feet and trying to sabotage my numbers. I'll go weeks at a time feeling exactly like this. So, yeah, a week doesn't even begin to cover it. It's been almost a year of living exactly like this, just constantly dialed in, seeing all the angles, and being ready to explode if someone gets in my way or tries to slow down my process.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods where you needed very little sleep—like just a few hours a night—but still felt completely wide awake and full of energy the next day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly, like I was saying earlier... it's exactly like that. It's like my body has just decided that three hours is a full night's rest, and honestly, with how much I get done before the sun even comes up, I really don't see the point of trying to force myself to sleep more anyway.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when your mood was so high, so irritable, or so intense that other people noticed a real change in you—one that lasted for at least a full week?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, you're asking if people noticed, and I guess... He showed me these reports that supposedly prove no one is tampering with anything, but I know what I see. I know they're manipulating the system behind my back. I know I touched on it a minute ago, but there have been plenty of weeks—entire stretches of time—where I sleep maybe three or four hours, max. And when I wake up, I am not groggy at all. I don't need to hit snooze, I don't need to drag myself to the coffee maker. I wake up feeling completely wired and ready to go. \n\nMy eyes just snap open, usually around 2:30 or 3:00 in the morning, and instantly, I'm thinking that lying in bed is just wasting time. I mean, I could use that time to make moves, right? Why would I just lay there staring at the ceiling in the dark when I have so much I could be doing? The wholesale market doesn't just stop because it's nighttime here. There are overseas suppliers I could be dealing with, price fluctuations I need to monitor, inventory logs I have to audit before my supervisor even gets to the office. \n\nEvery single minute I spend horizontal just feels like a missed opportunity, or worse, a chance for someone to slip something past me. So the second my eyes open and I feel that buzz—that intense, vibrating energy in my chest—I just know sleep is off the table. I'm wide awake, my brain is already ten steps ahead, and I just have to get up and get to my laptop. It's like my body has just decided that three hours is a full night's rest, and honestly, with how much I get done before the sun even comes up, I really don't see the point of trying to force myself to sleep more anyway.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when your mood was so high, so irritable, or so intense that other people noticed a real change in you—one that lasted for at least a full week?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, you're asking if people noticed, and I guess... I don't know, it's hard to say what other people really notice, right? Because they're all so wrapped up in their own little worlds, just doing their basic nine-to-five routines, checking boxes, clocking out. They don't have the same level of awareness that I do when I'm in that zone. But, I mean, did they see a change? I guess I haven't really paid that much attention to whether they were looking at me differently for a whole week straight. \n\nI know I told you about snapping at that logistics guy, and obviously, half the office saw that, so they noticed *that* moment, for sure. But as for them noticing a sustained change in my mood or my intensity for a full seven days... I don't know. People at work just seem to think I'm being demanding or difficult, or maybe they just think I'm really stressed about hitting my quotas for the quarter. They don't really come up to me and say, \"Hey, you've been acting incredibly elevated for the past week.\" They just kind of back off and give me a wide berth when I'm pacing around the breakroom or firing off emails at lightning speed. \n\nMy supervisor did tell me to take a breath once, but that was just a passing comment, not like a formal sit-down where he was tracking my mood for a week. And my family... well, I don't see them as much when I'm this dialed in because I'm just too busy. I have too much going on with the purchasing logs and the overseas vendors to just sit around making small talk with my mom or whatever. So, I don't really know if anyone has specifically clocked it as this week-long change in my whole personality. I'm just operating at a higher level, and if they notice anything, they probably just think I'm finally taking the job as seriously as they all *should* be taking it. So, no, I can't really say for sure if anyone has pointed it out like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that people were out to get you, or that you were being watched or followed for reasons that others might not understand?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, actually... that's exactly why I ended up coming in here in the first place. I mean, it's not just a feeling, it's this constant, heavy realization that I can't shake. It's like I'm walking around with a target on my back, and I know—I just know—that people at work, and even some of the overseas vendors I've been dealing with, are actively tracking my buys. \n\nAnd they're not just tracking them to be helpful or to monitor inventory. They are deliberately messing with the launch numbers. It's so obvious to me. They're altering the data, shifting the margins, just trying to make me look stupid in front of the whole department. I've spent hours going over the spreadsheets, looking at the timestamps, trying to catch the exact moment they go in and sabotage my work. It's like this massive, coordinated effort to undermine my authority and my process, because they know I'm onto them. They know I see the angles they're playing.\n\nI even took it to my supervisor. I printed out all these logs, I highlighted the discrepancies, I laid it all out on his desk. I was like, \"Look at this, look at what they're doing to my numbers!\" And he just... he looked at me like I was crazy. He pulled up the official reports on his screen and tried to tell me that the data didn't prove anything, that I was just misinterpreting the software updates or whatever excuse he came up with. He showed me these reports that supposedly prove no one is tampering with anything, but I know what I see. I know they're manipulating the system behind my back. It's just so frustrating because I can feel them watching my every move, waiting for me to slip up, and no matter how much evidence I gather, no one else seems to grasp the magnitude of what's actually happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that events, objects, or other people’s actions—like something you see on TV, in the news, or around you in daily life—have a special or hidden meaning specifically for you that others wouldn’t understand?\n\nPatient: No... no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I don't sit around thinking the news anchor is talking to me or that a billboard has some secret code on it. That's... that's not what's happening here. I'm talking about real things, you know? The data, the spreadsheets, the actual vendor communications. That's where the sabotage is. \n\nI don't need to look for hidden messages on TV because the messages I'm dealing with are right there in my inbox, clear as day, if you just know how to read the supply chain metrics. I mean, I watch the news sometimes, sure, like when I'm checking the overseas markets to see if there's a tariff change or a shipping delay that might affect my buys, but I'm looking at it for facts. I'm looking at the raw data of the global economy. I don't think the ticker at the bottom of the screen is spelling out my name or giving me instructions on how to reorganize the warehouse. \n\nIt's all very practical, very grounded in the reality of the wholesale market. The problem isn't that I'm seeing things that aren't there—it's that I'm the only one seeing the things that *are* there. The discrepancies in the launch numbers, the shifted margins, the timestamps on the log-ins... that's all real, tangible evidence of them messing with my work. So, no, I don't get distracted by random objects or TV shows having special meanings. I don't have time for that kind of nonsense when I have actual, documented proof of people actively trying to ruin my career right in front of me, even if my supervisor is too blind to see it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Special powers? I mean, I can't bend spoons with my mind or anything crazy like that, if that's what you're asking. But insights? Yeah, absolutely. A hundred percent. It’s like... it’s like my brain just unlocks this whole other level of understanding when it comes to the market. I get into this zone where everything just clicks, and I have this profound clarity that nobody else in the office possesses. 

Like, I’m technically still one of the junior buyers, right? I just have my high school diploma, I didn't go to some fancy business school like the senior guys, but when I get into that headspace, I start realizing that I can spot the next big trend before literally anyone else in L.A. even has a clue. It’s not just a guess, it’s this absolute certainty. I'll be looking at a new silhouette or an upcoming colorway from one of our vendors, and I don't just see a shoe—I can map out the exact cultural trajectory it’s going to take over the next eight months. I see the demand building before the public even knows it exists.

And it gets to the point where... well, it honestly feels like the whole sneaker market is basically just waiting on my decisions. Like the major brands, the resellers, the big boutiques downtown—they’re all just spinning their wheels, holding their breath until I make a call on what we’re stocking for the quarter. I see how the hype connects to the street level. So yeah, I guess you could call that an insight that no one else has, because they definitely don't see it. They just think I'm overstepping because of my junior title, but they don't understand that I'm literally driving the culture from my desk.

It's like my body has just decided that three hours is a full night's rest, and honestly, with how much I get done before the sun even comes up, I really don't see the point of trying to force myself to sleep more anyway.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when your mood was so high, so irritable, or so intense that other people noticed a real change in you—one that lasted for at least a full week?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, you're asking if people noticed, and I guess... I don't know, it's hard to say what other people really notice, right? Because they're all so wrapped up in their own little worlds, just doing their basic nine-to-five routines, checking boxes, clocking out. They don't have the same level of awareness that I do when I'm in that zone. But, I mean, did they see a change? I guess I haven't really paid that much attention to whether they were looking at me differently for a whole week straight. \n\nI know I told you about snapping at that logistics guy, and obviously, half the office saw that, so they noticed *that* moment, for sure. But as for them noticing a sustained change in my mood or my intensity for a full seven days... I don't know. People at work just seem to think I'm being demanding or difficult, or maybe they just think I'm really stressed about hitting my quotas for the quarter. They don't really come up to me and say, \"Hey, you've been acting incredibly elevated for the past week.\" They just kind of back off and give me a wide berth when I'm pacing around the breakroom or firing off emails at lightning speed. \n\nMy supervisor did tell me to take a breath once, but that was just a passing comment, not like a formal sit-down where he was tracking my mood for a week. And my family... well, I don't see them as much when I'm this dialed in because I'm just too busy. I have too much going on with the purchasing logs and the overseas vendors to just sit around making small talk with my mom or whatever. So, I don't really know if anyone has specifically clocked it as this week-long change in my whole personality. I'm just operating at a higher level, and if they notice anything, they probably just think I'm finally taking the job as seriously as they all *should* be taking it. So, no, I can't really say for sure if anyone has pointed it out like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that people were out to get you, or that you were being watched or followed for reasons that others might not understand?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, actually... that's exactly why I ended up coming in here in the first place. I mean, it's not just a feeling, it's this constant, heavy realization that I can't shake. It's like I'm walking around with a target on my back, and I know—I just know—that people at work, and even some of the overseas vendors I've been dealing with, are actively tracking my buys. \n\nAnd they're not just tracking them to be helpful or to monitor inventory. They are deliberately messing with the launch numbers. It's so obvious to me. They're altering the data, shifting the margins, just trying to make me look stupid in front of the whole department. I've spent hours going over the spreadsheets, looking at the timestamps, trying to catch the exact moment they go in and sabotage my work. It's like this massive, coordinated effort to undermine my authority and my process, because they know I'm onto them. They know I see the angles they're playing.\n\nI even took it to my supervisor. I printed out all these logs, I highlighted the discrepancies, I laid it all out on his desk. I was like, \"Look at this, look at what they're doing to my numbers!\" And he just... he looked at me like I was crazy. He pulled up the official reports on his screen and tried to tell me that the data didn't prove anything, that I was just misinterpreting the software updates or whatever excuse he came up with. He showed me these reports that supposedly prove no one is tampering with anything, but I know what I see. I know they're manipulating the system behind my back. It's just so frustrating because I can feel them watching my every move, waiting for me to slip up, and no matter how much evidence I gather, no one else seems to grasp the magnitude of what's actually happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that events, objects, or other people’s actions—like something you see on TV, in the news, or around you in daily life—have a special or hidden meaning specifically for you that others wouldn’t understand?\n\nPatient: No... no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I don't sit around thinking the news anchor is talking to me or that a billboard has some secret code on it. That's... that's not what's happening here. I'm talking about real things, you know? The data, the spreadsheets, the actual vendor communications. That's where the sabotage is. \n\nI don't need to look for hidden messages on TV because the messages I'm dealing with are right there in my inbox, clear as day, if you just know how to read the supply chain metrics. I mean, I watch the news sometimes, sure, like when I'm checking the overseas markets to see if there's a tariff change or a shipping delay that might affect my buys, but I'm looking at it for facts. I'm looking at the raw data of the global economy. I don't think the ticker at the bottom of the screen is spelling out my name or giving me instructions on how to reorganize the warehouse. \n\nIt's all very practical, very grounded in the reality of the wholesale market. The problem isn't that I'm seeing things that aren't there—it's that I'm the only one seeing the things that *are* there. The discrepancies in the launch numbers, the shifted margins, the timestamps on the log-ins... that's all real, tangible evidence of them messing with my work. So, no, I don't get distracted by random objects or TV shows having special meanings. I don't have time for that kind of nonsense when I have actual, documented proof of people actively trying to ruin my career right in front of me, even if my supervisor is too blind to see it.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed you had special powers, abilities, or insights that others don’t have—like you’re seeing the world in a way that no one else can?\n\nPatient: Special powers? I mean, I can't bend spoons with my mind or anything crazy like that, if that's what you're asking. But insights? Yeah, absolutely. A hundred percent. It’s like... it’s like my brain just unlocks this whole other level of understanding when it comes to the market. I get into this zone where everything just clicks, and I have this profound clarity that nobody else in the office possesses. \n\nLike, I’m technically still one of the junior buyers, right? I just have my high school diploma, I didn't go to some fancy business school like the senior guys, but when I get into that headspace, I start realizing that I can spot the next big trend before literally anyone else in L.A. even has a clue. It’s not just a guess, it’s this absolute certainty. I'll be looking at a new silhouette or an upcoming colorway from one of our vendors, and I don't just see a shoe—I can map out the exact cultural trajectory it’s going to take over the next eight months. I see the demand building before the public even knows it exists.\n\nAnd it gets to the point where... well, it honestly feels like the whole sneaker market is basically just waiting on my decisions. Like the major brands, the resellers, the big boutiques downtown—they’re all just spinning their wheels, holding their breath until I make a call on what we’re stocking for the quarter. I'll be sitting at my desk, firing through the catalogs, and I just know that if I pass on a certain drop, it's going to completely tank, but if I push for it, it's going to be the defining shoe of the season. The senior guys, they’re just looking at historical data, playing it safe, doing things the way they did five years ago, but I actually see the matrix of it all. I see how the hype connects to the street level. So yeah, I guess you could call that an insight that no one else has, because they definitely don't see it. They just think I'm overstepping because of my junior title, but they don't understand that I'm literally driving the culture from my desk.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

A mission? Like... like I'm chosen by destiny or something? No, man, that sounds like something out of a comic book or a movie. I'm not sitting here thinking the universe tapped me on the shoulder to, I don't know, lead a revolution or save humanity. It’s nothing weird or mystical like that. 

My focus is entirely on the industry. It's wholesale retail, it's apparel, it's footwear. I mean, don't get me wrong, I know my role is crucial, right? Like I just said, the market basically hinges on the calls I make, but that's just because I'm highly competent and I have that intense clarity I was telling you about. It's a skill. It's a deep, analytical insight into consumer behavior. It's not a "mission." A mission implies someone or something gave me a divine task, like I'm a prophet or whatever, and that’s just not me. 

I'm just a twenty-two-year-old guy who happens to understand supply and demand and cultural aesthetics way better than the guys sitting in the executive suites who have MBAs but no actual taste. I go to work, I look at the catalogs, I analyze the street-level trends, I deal with the vendors—even the ones actively trying to screw with my spreadsheets—and I make my buys. That's my day. I'm not trying to fulfill some grand prophecy. I just want to maximize our Q3 profits and make sure we aren't stuck with a warehouse full of deadstock just because some senior buyer thought a neon colorway was going to hit when anyone with eyes could see the trend was moving back to earth tones and vintage silhouettes. 

So, yeah, no special mission. No one "chosen" me for anything. I earned my spot, and I'm just doing my job at a level they can't even comprehend. And honestly, it takes up so much of my time and mental energy just trying to protect my launch numbers from being altered behind my back, like I mentioned before, that I wouldn't even have the time for some kind of grand cosmic mission even if one existed. I'm just trying to keep the supply chain moving and keep these people from ruining my career.

I mean, that's nothing. That is just a drop in the bucket compared to what I've actually been dealing with. It's funny you ask if it's just a week, because when I really sit down and look at the timeline—and believe me, I have mapped this out in my head a hundred times while I'm pacing around my living room at 4:00 AM—it goes back way, way further than that. \n\nHonestly, these... these elevated, irritable, high-energy stretches have been part of my life for about eleven months. Eleven months! It's not just some quick little phase where I have a good week and then it's over, or a couple days of being annoyed. It's been nearly a whole year of this. I can actually trace it back to last spring, right around the time I was putting together this incredibly complex bulk order for smart home hubs, and I just felt this... this massive switch flip inside me. \n\nAnd ever since then, for those eleven months, it's just been these long, drawn-out periods where I am completely vibrating with all this power and clarity, and then getting so incredibly frustrated because I have to deal with everyone else dragging their feet and trying to sabotage my numbers. I'll go weeks at a time feeling exactly like this. So, yeah, a week doesn't even begin to cover it. It's been almost a year of living exactly like this, just constantly dialed in, seeing all the angles, and being ready to explode if someone gets in my way or tries to slow down my process.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods where you needed very little sleep—like just a few hours a night—but still felt completely wide awake and full of energy the next day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly, like I was saying earlier... it's exactly like that. I know I touched on it a minute ago, but there have been plenty of weeks—entire stretches of time—where I sleep maybe three or four hours, max. And when I wake up, I am not groggy at all. I don't need to hit snooze, I don't need to drag myself to the coffee maker. I wake up feeling completely wired and ready to go. \n\nMy eyes just snap open, usually around 2:30 or 3:00 in the morning, and instantly, I'm thinking that lying in bed is just wasting time. I mean, I could use that time to make moves, right? Why would I just lay there staring at the ceiling in the dark when I have so much I could be doing? The wholesale market doesn't just stop because it's nighttime here. There are overseas suppliers I could be dealing with, price fluctuations I need to monitor, inventory logs I have to audit before my supervisor even gets to the office. \n\nEvery single minute I spend horizontal just feels like a missed opportunity, or worse, a chance for someone to slip something past me. So the second my eyes open and I feel that buzz—that intense, vibrating energy in my chest—I just know sleep is off the table. I'm wide awake, my brain is already ten steps ahead, and I just have to get up and get to my laptop. It's like my body has just decided that three hours is a full night's rest, and honestly, with how much I get done before the sun even comes up, I really don't see the point of trying to force myself to sleep more anyway.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when your mood was so high, so irritable, or so intense that other people noticed a real change in you—one that lasted for at least a full week?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, you're asking if people noticed, and I guess... I don't know, it's hard to say what other people really notice, right? Because they're all so wrapped up in their own little worlds, just doing their basic nine-to-five routines, checking boxes, clocking out. They don't have the same level of awareness that I do when I'm in that zone. But, I mean, did they see a change? I guess I haven't really paid that much attention to whether they were looking at me differently for a whole week straight. \n\nI know I told you about snapping at that logistics guy, and obviously, half the office saw that, so they noticed *that* moment, for sure. But as for them noticing a sustained change in my mood or my intensity for a full seven days... I don't know. People at work just seem to think I'm being demanding or difficult, or maybe they just think I'm really stressed about hitting my quotas for the quarter. They don't really come up to me and say, \"Hey, you've been acting incredibly elevated for the past week.\" They just kind of back off and give me a wide berth when I'm pacing around the breakroom or firing off emails at lightning speed. \n\nMy supervisor did tell me to take a breath once, but that was just a passing comment, not like a formal sit-down where he was tracking my mood for a week. And my family... well, I don't see them as much when I'm this dialed in because I'm just too busy. I have too much going on with the purchasing logs and the overseas vendors to just sit around making small talk with my mom or whatever. So, I don't really know if anyone has specifically clocked it as this week-long change in my whole personality. I'm just operating at a higher level, and if they notice anything, they probably just think I'm finally taking the job as seriously as they all *should* be taking it. So, no, I can't really say for sure if anyone has pointed it out like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that people were out to get you, or that you were being watched or followed for reasons that others might not understand?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, actually... that's exactly why I ended up coming in here in the first place. I mean, it's not just a feeling, it's this constant, heavy realization that I can't shake. It's like I'm walking around with a target on my back, and I know—I just know—that people at work, and even some of the overseas vendors I've been dealing with, are actively tracking my buys. \n\nAnd they're not just tracking them to be helpful or to monitor inventory. They are deliberately messing with the launch numbers. It's so obvious to me. They're altering the data, shifting the margins, just trying to make me look stupid in front of the whole department. I've spent hours going over the spreadsheets, looking at the timestamps, trying to catch the exact moment they go in and sabotage my work. It's like this massive, coordinated effort to undermine my authority and my process, because they know I'm onto them. They know I see the angles they're playing.\n\nI even took it to my supervisor. I printed out all these logs, I highlighted the discrepancies, I laid it all out on his desk. I was like, \"Look at this, look at what they're doing to my numbers!\" And he just... he looked at me like I was crazy. He pulled up the official reports on his screen and tried to tell me that the data didn't prove anything, that I was just misinterpreting the software updates or whatever excuse he came up with. He showed me these reports that supposedly prove no one is tampering with anything, but I know what I see. I know they're manipulating the system behind my back. It's just so frustrating because I can feel them watching my every move, waiting for me to slip up, and no matter how much evidence I gather, no one else seems to grasp the magnitude of what's actually happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that events, objects, or other people’s actions—like something you see on TV, in the news, or around you in daily life—have a special or hidden meaning specifically for you that others wouldn’t understand?\n\nPatient: No... no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I don't sit around thinking the news anchor is talking to me or that a billboard has some secret code on it. That's... that's not what's happening here. I'm talking about real things, you know? The data, the spreadsheets, the actual vendor communications. That's where the sabotage is. \n\nI don't need to look for hidden messages on TV because the messages I'm dealing with are right there in my inbox, clear as day, if you just know how to read the supply chain metrics. I mean, I watch the news sometimes, sure, like when I'm checking the overseas markets to see if there's a tariff change or a shipping delay that might affect my buys, but I'm looking at it for facts. I'm looking at the raw data of the global economy. I don't think the ticker at the bottom of the screen is spelling out my name or giving me instructions on how to reorganize the warehouse. \n\nIt's all very practical, very grounded in the reality of the wholesale market. The problem isn't that I'm seeing things that aren't there—it's that I'm the only one seeing the things that *are* there. The discrepancies in the launch numbers, the shifted margins, the timestamps on the log-ins... that's all real, tangible evidence of them messing with my work. So, no, I don't get distracted by random objects or TV shows having special meanings. I don't have time for that kind of nonsense when I have actual, documented proof of people actively trying to ruin my career right in front of me, even if my supervisor is too blind to see it.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed you had special powers, abilities, or insights that others don’t have—like you’re seeing the world in a way that no one else can?\n\nPatient: Special powers? I mean, I can't bend spoons with my mind or anything crazy like that, if that's what you're asking. But insights? Yeah, absolutely. A hundred percent. It’s like... it’s like my brain just unlocks this whole other level of understanding when it comes to the market. I get into this zone where everything just clicks, and I have this profound clarity that nobody else in the office possesses. \n\nLike, I’m technically still one of the junior buyers, right? I just have my high school diploma, I didn't go to some fancy business school like the senior guys, but when I get into that headspace, I start realizing that I can spot the next big trend before literally anyone else in L.A. even has a clue. It’s not just a guess, it’s this absolute certainty. I'll be looking at a new silhouette or an upcoming colorway from one of our vendors, and I don't just see a shoe—I can map out the exact cultural trajectory it’s going to take over the next eight months. I see the demand building before the public even knows it exists.\n\nAnd it gets to the point where... well, it honestly feels like the whole sneaker market is basically just waiting on my decisions. Like the major brands, the resellers, the big boutiques downtown—they’re all just spinning their wheels, holding their breath until I make a call on what we’re stocking for the quarter. I'll be sitting at my desk, firing through the catalogs, and I just know that if I pass on a certain drop, it's going to completely tank, but if I push for it, it's going to be the defining shoe of the season. The senior guys, they’re just looking at historical data, playing it safe, doing things the way they did five years ago, but I actually see the matrix of it all. I see how the hype connects to the street level. So yeah, I guess you could call that an insight that no one else has, because they definitely don't see it. They just think I'm overstepping because of my junior title, but they don't understand that I'm literally driving the culture from my desk.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that you had a special role or mission that no one else could fulfill, or that you were chosen for something bigger than yourself?\n\nPatient: A mission? Like... like I'm chosen by destiny or something? No, man, that sounds like something out of a comic book or a movie. I'm not sitting here thinking the universe tapped me on the shoulder to, I don't know, lead a revolution or save humanity. It’s nothing weird or mystical like that. \n\nMy focus is entirely on the industry. It's wholesale retail, it's apparel, it's footwear. I mean, don't get me wrong, I know my role is crucial, right? Like I just said, the market basically hinges on the calls I make, but that's just because I'm highly competent and I have that intense clarity I was telling you about. It's a skill. It's a deep, analytical insight into consumer behavior. It's not a \"mission.\" A mission implies someone or something gave me a divine task, like I'm a prophet or whatever, and that’s just not me. \n\nI'm just a twenty-two-year-old guy who happens to understand supply and demand and cultural aesthetics way better than the guys sitting in the executive suites who have MBAs but no actual taste. I go to work, I look at the catalogs, I analyze the street-level trends, I deal with the vendors—even the ones actively trying to screw with my spreadsheets—and I make my buys. That's my day. I'm not trying to fulfill some grand prophecy. I just want to maximize our Q3 profits and make sure we aren't stuck with a warehouse full of deadstock just because some senior buyer thought a neon colorway was going to hit when anyone with eyes could see the trend was moving back to earth tones and vintage silhouettes. \n\nSo, yeah, no special mission. No one \"chosen\" me for anything. I earned my spot, and I'm just doing my job at a level they can't even comprehend. And honestly, it takes up so much of my time and mental energy just trying to protect my launch numbers from being altered behind my back, like I mentioned before, that I wouldn't even have the time for some kind of grand cosmic mission even if one existed. I'm just trying to keep the supply chain moving and keep these people from ruining my career.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Down? Hopeless? No, I don't... I don't really get like that. I mean, sure, I get frustrated, right? Like anyone would when they're dealing with the kind of sheer incompetence I see every single day at the office. But losing interest? Not functioning? That's just... that's completely impossible for me. I literally can't afford to lose interest. I'm so wired into the work, so focused on making sure the buys are perfect and the inventory is flawless, that I couldn't stop caring even if I tried. It’s just constant motion, constant analysis for me. Like, if I took two weeks where I couldn't function normally? That would mean missing an entire seasonal drop window. If I did that, the downtown boutiques would eat us alive, our competitors would scoop up all the exclusive colorways, and we'd be stuck with last season's deadstock clogging up the warehouse. I would never let that happen. Ever. So, no, I don't get down or lose interest. I'm just too driven, too focused on the logistics and the culture to ever feel hopeless about anything. I have too much on my plate to even entertain the idea of slowing down.
